Aris stared at her, trembling with panic. He didn't say another word. He turned and stumbled against the door, and ran down the hallway like a rat fleeing a sinking ship.

The office went silent.

Paula stood in the center of the room. The cardboard box sat on the desk behind her. The adrenaline that had sustained her through the Dean's office and the confrontation with Aris suddenly vanished, leaving her body cold and entirely devoid of strength.

Her knees buckled.

She sank straight down onto the floor, her back sliding against the bookcase, her knees pulled up to her chest.

She had done it. She had saved Sophie. She had destroyed Aris. She had cut out the rot of the blackmail and thrown it in the fire.

She was free.

So why did it feel like she was dying?

Paula buried her face in her hands, her shoulders shaking as the dam finally broke.

She didn't cry quietly, she sobbed, She was mad at herself, furious that she had let it get this far, disgusted by her own cowardice, and devastated by the crushing realization that she had just shut the door on her entire identity.

She sat there on the floor for over an hour, crying until her eyes were swollen and her throat was raw, mourning the twenty five years she had just burned to the ground.

And underneath all of it hurting the most.. Sophie isn't here.

She had won the war, she had protected the girl, and now she was permanently alone.

She had brought this on herself. Who was she kidding? She deserved every single piece of the ruin.

Eventually, Paula forced herself off the floor. Her joints ached as if she had been beaten.

She taped the cardboard box shut, grabbed her coat, and left her office for the very last time.

She didn't look back. She told campus security that she would have a courier service collect the remaining files by Friday.

She drove home in absolute silence, the radio off, the windows rolled up against the warm May air.

When she walked into the penthouse, the space felt like a tomb. It wasn't the clean, elegant minimalism she had curated over a decade, it was just empty. Cold. Lifeless.

Paula walked straight to the kitchen bar cart. She unscrewed the heavy crystal stopper of a top shelf Kentucky bourbon, brought the bottle to her lips, and drank.

It burned like liquid fire all the way down. She walked into the living room, kicked off her heels, and collapsed onto the sofa. She took another long, burning sip from the bottle.

The alcohol hit her empty stomach, making her head spin immediately.

She didn't want to think about the Board. She didn't want to think about the Dean, or Aris, or the undergraduate grants, or New York, or August.

She just wanted to stop feeling the massive hole in her chest where Sophie used to live.

Paula drank. She drank until the ceiling started to tilt, until the burning in her throat numbed out, until the memory of Sophie's face was gone.

By 9:00 PM, the bottle was half empty, and Paula’s body could no longer support its own weight.

She slipped sideways off the sofa, landing heavily on the rug. The bourbon bottle rolled from her limp fingers.

Paula didn't move. She curled onto her side on the floor, pulling her knees up to her chest rocking.

She passed out cold on the floor of her own empty kingdom.

The phone on the kitchen island had been vibrating for an hour. But Paula was passed out. Her face pressed against the living room rug.

The phone stopped. Then, three seconds later, it started again.

Bzzzz. Bzzzz. Bzzzz.

Down in the lobby Clara was standing by the desk, her fingers tapping a nervously in the counter.

"I'm telling you, Arthur, she's not answering her cell, she's not answering her landline, and her office at the university said she cleared out her desk at five o'clock," Clara said, leaning across the desk.

Arthur, the veteran doorman who had known Dr. Martin a long time hesitated. He looked at Clara, then down at the master key ring hanging on its hook behind the counter.

"You know the protocol," Arthur said, rubbing the back of his neck. "Residents' privacy. Unless there's an active medical emergency…"

"This is a medical emergency, Arthur," Clara yelled, pulling out her driver's license and sliding it across the counter alongside a fifty-dollar bill. "Look at my credentials. Look at my face. If I walk up to that penthouse and she’s fine, then I’m sorry. But if I go up there and she’s swallowed a bottle of sleeping pills because her entire life just imploded, and you made me stand here arguing about protocol while she asphyxiated..I’ll sue!"

Arthur swallowed hard, glanced at the fifty, and sighed. He reached for the key ring. “Clara. If she yells at me, I'm telling her you held me hostage."

"You're a saint, Arthur," Clara snapped, snatching the key from the hook.

She hit the service stairs, taking them two at a time. Her chest was burning by the time she reached the fourteenth floor, but she didn't care.

She jammed the key into the lock, turned it, and pushed the door open.

"Paula?" Clara called out, stepping into the apartment. "Paula, if you're hiding in here drinking your weight in pinot noir because you're having an existential crisis about a twenty-three-year-old dancer, I am going to physically beat you."

No answer.

Clara kicked off her heels and walked further in.

That was when she saw her.

Paula was lying crumpled near the edge of the floor infront of her couch, her legs pulled up toward her chest in a fetal position.

. Her face was deathly pale. “Jesus Christ, Paula," Clara said.

She dropped to her knees beside her friend, sliding her hands under Paula’s shoulders.

"Paula! Paula, wake up. Can you hear me?"

Paula’s eyes fluttered, but they didn't focus. A low groan escaped her throat. She tried to pull away. "Go away..." she slurred. "Leave me... just leave me..."

"I'm not leaving you, you dramatic idiot," Clara said, though her hands were shaking as she tried to pick Paula up. "Get up. Come on. Help me out here. Use your legs."

It took ten minutes before Clara managed to drag Paula out of the living room and down thehallway toward the master bathroom.

Paula was dead weight, her eyes rolling back, her body trembling with a mixture of alcohol poisoning and nervous shock.

Clara shoved her into the shower, leaving Paula sitting slumped.

Without hesitating, Clara reached out and cranked the handle all the way to cold.

Freezing water hit Paula in the chest. "Agh!" Paula yelled, yelling as if she had been stabbed.

Her eyes flew wide open, wild and bloodshot, her hands flying up to push at the spray. "What are you doin?! Are you insane?!"

"Waking you up, you suicidal maniac," Clara snapped, standing outside the glass door with her arms crossed.

Paula sat under the freezing water for a minute. The initial shock wore off, replaced by a miserable clarity.

She dragged a wet hand down her face, wiping the water from her eyes, and stared at floor between her feet.

Clara reached in, turned it to warm and stepped inside with her clothes on, sitting down on the tile bench next to her friend.

"Talk to me," Clara said concerned. " I called looking for you to day at your office, they said you cleared your office? Please tell me you didn’t do that Paula?”

Paula stared at the wall. "It's true," Paula whispered. "I resigned."

Clara closed her eyes, letting out a long sigh. "Oh, Paula... why? Did Aris go to the board? Did he them about you?"

"No," Paula shook her head.

"Then why resign? If Aris didn't expose you..."

"Because I exposed myself," Paula interrupted. She turned her head to look at Clara, her brown eyes teary. "I couldn't live one more minute being a prisoner to a man who uses students as leverage. I walked into Harrington’s office. I confessed to everything. The relationship, the abuse of power, the entire sordid mess."

Clara stared at her, stunned. "You told the Dean? Voluntarily?"

"I had to," Paula sobbed still clearly tipsy.

"Sophie is graduating in three weeks. She has a major contract waiting for her in New York. If Aris had used the blackmail, or if an investigation had blown up publicly, they would have dragged her through the mud. They would have flagged her transcript, ruined her visa, destroyed her entire future, I had to fall on the sword, Clara. It was the only way to guarantee Harrington would seal the file. I traded my twenty-five years, my tenure, my pension, my entire identity... just to ensure she could graduate with a clean record."

"And Aris?" Clara asked. "What happened to Aris?"

"I destroyed him on my way out the door," Paula said almost smiling. "I gave Harrington the exact paper trail of his extortion. The Dean is launching a forensic audit of his lab starting Monday morning. He’s finished. But that doesn't fix me. Look at me, Clara. I’m fifty years old. I have no job, no office, no routine, and no purpose. I am standing in the wreckage of a life I built with my own hands."

"You did a noble thing, Paula," Clara said gently, wrapping an arm around Paula’s shoulders and pulling her close against her.

"You protected a young woman from the collateral damage of your own mistakes. That isn't weakness. That's taking responsibility."

"Don't," Paula choked out, burying her face in Clara’s shoulder. "Don't make it sound noble. It hurts too much. It just feels stupid. I traded everything... and for what? For a girl who hates me."

Clara rubbed circles into Paula's back, letting her cry. "She doesn't hate you, Paula."

"She does," Paula cried. "When she walked out of my office weeks ago, she looked at me like I was a monster. And she’s right. I threatened to throw her under the bus. I told her she was a liability. I used every cruel, word in my vocabulary to push her away because I was terrified."

"Then tell her," Clara said. "Call her. Tell her what you did today. Tell her you gave up your entire career so she could be ok."

Paula shook her head. "No. Never."

"Paula, are you insane? She deserves to know."

"She must never know," Paula interrupted. "Do you understand me, Clara? If Sophie knows I resigned to protect her, she will feel indebted. She will try to stay. She’ll throw away her Broadway contract out of some misplaced sense of loyalty or guilt. I won't let her anchor herself to a ruined, fifty-year-old woman with no future. She has to go to New York thinking I am a cold, selfish, cowardly hypocrite. She has to hate me enough to leave. That is the final price of this mistake. I will not let her sacrifice her life for mine twice."

Clara looked at her friend and felt a profound sorrow.

Paula Martin had spent her entire life analyzing human behavior, controlling variables, and engineering outcomes. But sitting here, soaked to the bone on her shower floor, having torched her entire empire for a twenty three year old dancer, Paula had finally learned what it meant to love someone more than she loved herself.

"You're a stubborn, brilliant, catastrophic fool," Clara whispered, a smile on her lips as she pulled Paula back into her arms.

"I know," Paula said quietly, letting the hot water wash over them both as her heart hurt more. "I know."

——-

In Sophie’s dorm, bags were packed. Their flights to New York were booked for next Tuesday.

Exactly one week.

One week until Manchester was just a blurry memory for her.

Chad was over at Sophie’s.

Sophie was sitting on the floor, staring down at a pair of worn black heels she hadn't worn since the night at the club when Paula pulled her in that room . Her heart hurting from the absence of Paula.

."We’re going out tonight."

Chad froze. He blinked, looking down at her. "Tonight? As in... tonight-tonight? The night before graduation? When we have to wake up at seven in the morning to put on those ridiculous robes and sit in a stifling auditorium for four hours while some donor talks about endowment funds?"

"Yes, tonight-tonight," Sophie said, standing up and brushing her hands off on her jeans.

"It's our last night. One more time. We're going wherever, we're getting hammered, and we're acting like normal 23 year olds who don't have dread about boarding a jet to JFK in seven days."

Chad stared at her for a second. He knew why she wanted to go out. She wanted to stop thinking about what had happened with Paula.

When she started thinking about her she couldn’t sit in her dorm and do nothing.

"Fine," Chad sighed. "We go out, but if you leave me for another girl, I’ll ignore you when we get to New York!"

The bar wasn't fancy. It was full of people who were about to graduate. Celebrating.

Sophie stood near the corner of the bar, a mojito in her hand feeling the music in her chest. She was wearing a short black dress, her hair tumbling over her shoulders in loose, untamed waves. She didn't look like a student anymore, she looked dangerous, and completely untouchable.

"Are you gonna stare at that drink all night, or are you actually gonna dance with someone?"

Sophie turned her head. A girl was standing next to her, leaning against the bar with a confident, easy smirk.

She was younger, probably a freshman, maybe nineteen, with big green eyes. She was wearing a small denim jacket over a graphic tee, jeans and heels. Her posture relaxed in a way Sophie hadn't seen in a while.

"I’ll let you know when I figure it out," Sophie said with a smile.

The girl laughed. "Right. I'm Maya. Freshman modern dance program."

"Sophie. Senior contemporary and jazz," Sophie replied, clinking her glass against Maya's.

They talked for an hour. It was easy. Maya wasn’t deep or too intellectual, which was kind of a turn off to what Sophie had known for a while. To Maya, Sophie was just this mythical senior creature, someone who had survived the arts department and was actually packing bags for New York.

"Is it terrifying?" Maya asked "Moving out there? I heard they go hard on you.”

"They do," Sophie said honestly, looking down at her drink. "I hear they chew you up, spit you out, and ask if you can do a double pirouette on the way down. But if you love it enough, you just... keep chewing back."

Maya stared at her, completely captivated.

They talked about the teachers. The classes. The accomplishments Sophie got with the summer sessions and the auditions. Maya leaned in "You're kind of incredible, you know that?"

Sophie smiled. She wasn’t super turned on by her, not like she was with Paula, but she needed to learn that other people out there aren’t like Paula but can be good for her.

"Come on," Sophie whispered, setting her glass down. "Let's dance."

Maya was a good dancer. Sophie could tell she was only going to get better. It felt like looking into a mirror of Sophie's past self, before her entire world got complicated.

When Maya reached out, her hands rested on Sophie’s hips, Sophie let her pull her close.

By 2:00 AM, they were back in Sophie’s room. As soon as the door shut, Maya backed Sophie up against the wall, kissing her. It was so different than what Sophie was used to.

Sophie kissed her back, sliding her hands under Maya’s jacket and shirt, feeling the warmth of her skin. It started slow, but in true Sophie fashion, . The gentleness was short lived. Sophie grabbed Maya’s wrists and held them.

Maya was surprised by the sudden shift in aggression, but she didn't pull away.

She matched Sophie’s energy leaning into her touch as Sophie kissed down her neck, biting just hard enough to leave a mark.

“Is this ok?” Sophie asked as she kissed down her back taking off Maya shirt.

“Mmmhmm.” Maya said enjoying Sophie all over her.

Sophie stumbled and walked her to the bed. Maya laid back and Sophie crawled on her. They made out for a while. Hands roaming. Clothes coming off.

Sophie dominated her. She pulled her pants off and pushed in to her. She cried out. Clearly new at someone being so aggressive.

Sophie liked doing this to her. The others had been just a thing to feel a void. And maybe it was because Maya was inexperienced and young. But it felt good.

They countinued for a while. Sophie showed Maya things she probably never felt before. And she loved it.

Later, Maya was fell asleep, laying on against Sophie’s collarbone.

Sophie lay wide awake in the dark, staring up at the ceiling.

She turned her head, looking at the blonde hair laying across her pillow. Maya was sweet. She was pretty. She was everything a normal 24 year old should be hooking up with the night before graduation.

And Sophie felt absolutely nothing. She wanted Paula. She needed Paula.

In a week, she’d be thousands of miles away. A whole new life. It broke her heart into pieces because she knew that she was never, ever going to get over Paula Martin.

Sophie closed her eyes, letting out a quiet, sigh, and waited for the sun to come up.

——-

The graduation ceremony was a blur. And packed.

Chad found Sophie backstage just before they lined up for the processional. He looked happy in his cap and gown

Sophie was wearing her black robes, her hair straight, her eyes constantly darting toward the faculty seating section off to the side of the stage.

Usually, the senior faculty sat in the front rows, wearing their doctoral hoods, scarlet for divinity, purple for law, dark blue for philosophy. And right there, near the center, should be Dr. Paula Martin, the distinguished chair of the department, looking like an absolute queen of academia.

They filed out to the sound of Pomp and Circumstance. The crowd cheered. Parents taking pictures and yelling at their child.

Sophie took her seat in the second row. She scanned the rows of seated professors.

Aris.

There he was, looking smug in his dark blue hood.

The Dean. A smile on his face.

Paula wasn't there.

She leaned to the left, trying to see around the shoulder of the kid sitting next to her.

Empty chair.

She looked back at the stage. Listening to the Dean talk.

A little later she stiff in line for her diploma. Still looking where the professors were. Wondering where Paula had been.

“Sophie Webster!"

Sophie walked across the stage..

She saw the Dean standing there, holding out a leather folder. He smiled, his hand extending.

"Congratulations, Ms. Webster," he said, his voice dry and professional. "A bright future ahead of you."

"Thank you," Sophie said, taking the folder.

She shook his hand and walked across the stage, forcing a smile for the university photographer whose flash went off right in her face.

Click.

She walked down the opposite stairs, and waved to her family.

After the ceremony ended, everyone joined their family taking pics and smiling.

Chad ran over and grabbed Sophie. Picked her up.

"We did it!" Chad yelled, spinning her around before setting her down then running to his family.

Sophie smiled, letting her family, her mom, her dad, her younger sister, drown her with hugs and flowers.

They took pictures by the fountain. They told her how proud they were. They asked her what time they were all heading to dinner.

"Go ahead," Sophie said, forcing a smile for her mother. "Can you get the table and I’ll be right there. Chad and I just need to... I left something in the arts wing. I'll meet you there in twenty minutes."

"Don't be long, sweetie! The reservation is for six!" her mom called out, distracted by Chad’s dad holding up a phone for a group shot.

Sophie left.

She didn't go to the arts wing. She went straight to the West Academic Building.

The hallways were empty. Everyone was out in the quad celebrating. She walked past the classrooms, and stopped right in front of Paula’s office door.

Dr. Paula R. Martin. Department Chair.

The nameplate was still there. But the door was locked. The blinds were pulled all the way down.

Sophie raised her hand to knock, but before she could, she heard footsteps from down the hall.

She turned. It was her old Calculus professor, carrying papers under his arm.

"Oh. Hello, Sophie. Congratulations on the graduation," he said politely.

"Thank you.. Professor," Sophie blurted out, taking a step toward him. "Quick question, do you know where Dr. Martin is? She wasn't at the ceremony."

The professor’s polite smile left. He shifted the stack of papers in his arms, his expression turning awkward.

"Oh," he said softly. "Well guess you don’t know."

Sophie interrupted . "Know what?"

"She resigned, dear," the professor said. "A couple weeks ago. Handed her resignation right to the Dean's office before anyone even knew what was happening. Took early retirement, packed up her office by the end of that week."

Sophie stood there. The words didn't make sense.

"Resigned?" Sophie repeated.

"Well maybe administrative burn out, I suppose," the professor said, clearly uncomfortable discussing departmental gossip with a graduating student. "Happens to the best of them. Anyway... congratulations again, Sophie. Have a wonderful summer."

He walked past her.

So stood alone in front of the door.

Resigned.

Sophie didn’t know how to feel.

By 8:30 PM, the family dinner was over.

Sophie had smiled, eaten three bites of salmon, answered questions about New York, and smiled through her dad’s toast.

They said their goodbyes. The made plans to fly and see her show in NY.

The moment they dropped her off at the dorm and said goodbye Sophie left her dorm and took a cab to Paula’s apartment.

The cab ride took 10 minutes.

When the cab pulled up to the building, Sophie jumped out, handing the driver a twenty without waiting for change. She pushed through the revolving doors.

Arthur, the doorman, was standing behind the concierge desk. He looked up, as she got On the elevator.

Sophie knocked Paula’s door. She stood there. Nervous. Fixing her hair. She knocked again. Silence.

She felt defeated. So she walked back down to the front desk.

“Everything ok Ms.?” He said to Sophie as she walked up to him.

“Uh yeah. Can you get a message to Paula Martin with she returns. Tell that Sop..”

He interrupted. “I’m sorry Ms., Ms. Martin isn’t here anymore.”

Sophie looked confused. "What do you mean, she’s not anymore? Is she out of town?"

"She cleared out her place on Tuesday," Arthur said quietly. "Left her car in the rental garage, but she’s staying with a friend for a bit while she figures out her next move."

Sophie didn't talk. She turned around, walked out the glass doors into the night, and started walking.

She walked for ten blocks, crying so hard she couldn't catch her breath. She was going to NY and never going to see Paula again.

——-

Sophie stared up at the green letters, her duffel bag strap digging in to her shoulder.

Her entire life had been compressed into a large duffel bag and 1 checked luggage.

Her eyes kept darting toward the security entrance. Every time a tall woman in a dark coat walked past, her heart skipped.

There was little voice screaming that Paula was going to come running through the doors. That Paula was gonna grab her and beg her to stay. Or beg to come with her.

The final boarding call for flight 404 to JFK echoed through speakers.

Nobody came through the doors. Just business travelers, and crying babies.

"Come on." Chad said softly, his usual sarcastic self stopped. Noticing the emotions on Sophie’s face.

He wrapped an arm around her shoulders, pulling her close. "She's not coming, Soph. Let's go conquer New York."

Sophie took one last look at the doors, and took a deep breath.

She wished Paula would come running through. She honestly did. She wanted Paula to show up, begging Sophie to love her forever.

But it never happened. The plane left. Her and Chad were on the way to the states. And Paula Martin would be a memory.

Chad fell asleep almost immediately, snoring softly with his head lolled against the seat.

Sophie stayed wide awake. She stared out the small window into the black void of the Atlantic Ocean, and she thought about Paula.

She wondered where she was. She wondered if Paula felt the painful distance , the way Sophie did.

She wanted to text her. Call her. But she couldn’t. She wouldn’t. So Sophie let it go.
